**** LINES, CONNECTIONS, AND METERS ****
§ 51.01 COLLECTION OF COSTS FOR SERVICE LINES.
   On all streets, avenues, and alleys where the service lines have been laid
to the curb, the cost of such lines shall be collected from the property owners
before the water is turned on.
(Prior Code, § 933.01) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926)
§ 51.02 INDIVIDUAL SERVICE LINES FOR APARTMENTS.
   Each and every apartment in any dwelling house or other building to be
supplied with water shall have its own service line to the main line.
(Prior Code, § 933.02) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.03 TAP-IN CHARGE.
   There will be a charge for new tap-ins to the main water service which
charge shall be determined by the Borough Engineer at the time of the tap-in.
(Prior Code, § 933.04) (Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003)
§ 51.04 APPLICATION FOR SERVICE FROM OLD CONNECTION.
   Where water connection has previously been installed and water is desired, a
proper application shall be signed by the owner or landlord.
(Prior Code, § 933.05) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926)
§ 51.05 APPLICATION FOR SERVICE FROM NEW CONNECTION.
   (A)   Service connection shall be made upon written application on a blank
prepared by the borough for the purpose signed by the owner, or his or her
properly authorized agent, and water shall be furnished upon written
application signed either by the owner, his or her duly authorized agent, or by
a tenant, except as otherwise noted, and after approval of such application by
the borough is endorsed thereon.
   (B)   A new application must be made and approved by the borough upon any
change in ownership of property, in tenancy or in the service, as described in
the application, and the borough shall have the right, upon five days’ notice,
to discontinue the water supply until such new application has been made and
approved.
(Prior Code, § 933.06) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.06 CHARGES FOR REINSTALLATION OF METERS.
   (A)   The charges for reinstallation of meters, when removed because of
damage in any way for which the customer is responsible either because of
willful deed or negligence, shall be as set from time to time by resolution of
Council and cost of parts. This cost shall be as set from time to time by
resolution of Council for meters one inch in diameter and smaller, plus the
cost of any parts, and as set from time to time by resolution of Council
additional per one-half inch in diameter, or fraction thereof for meters larger
than one inch, which charge shall include testing and shall be paid in advance.
   (B)   When the meters are removed after installation, at the request of the
customer for testing, the following rules shall apply.
      (1)   If the meter tested shall be found to be accurate within the limits
herein specified, a fee determined for the schedule in division (B)(2) below,
shall be paid to the borough by the customer requiring such test; but if not so
found, then the cost thereof shall be borne by the borough.
      (2)   The amount of fee shall be as set from time to time by resolution
of Council for each water service meter having an outlet not exceeding one
inch. For other water service meters having an outlet not exceeding two inches,
the test fee shall be as set from time to time by resolution of Council per
meter. The charge for request test of meters above two inches shall be at the
rate as set from time to time by resolution of Council per inch of diameter
outlet.
(Prior Code, § 933.07) (Ord. 458, passed 8-19-1968; Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003)
§ 51.07 CONNECTIONS; MAINTENANCE OF LINES AND METERS.
   (A)   The borough shall furnish, place, and maintain, at its own cost, the
ferrule and coupling at the connection of the service line and the main.
   (B)   The service line, which shall be of copper or other appropriate
material, including the curb stop and box, shall be furnished, laid, and
maintained by the applicant at his or her own cost, subject to the rules of the
borough.
   (C)   All connections, service lines, and fixtures furnished by the
applicant shall be maintained by him or her in good order, and all valves,
meters, and appliances furnished and owned by the borough and on the property
of the applicant shall be properly protected and cared for by the applicant.
All leaks in the service line or any other pipe or fixture in or upon the
premises supplied must be immediately repaired by the owner or occupant of the
premises.
   (D)   All pipes, meters, and fixtures shall, at all reasonable hours, be
subject to the inspection by proper employees of the borough. No addition or
alteration in or about any connection with the main or pipe system on the
premises shall be made by anyone without first notifying the borough and
preparing an amended application made for such purpose. This rule shall not
apply to changes in pipes where no new fixtures or outlets are contemplated.
   (E)   The borough shall in no event be responsible for maintaining any
portion of the service line owned by the applicant or for damage done by water
escaping therefrom or for lines or fixtures on the applicant’s property. The
applicant shall at all times comply with municipal regulations with reference
thereto and shall make changes therein required on account of changes of grade,
relocation of mains, or otherwise.
   (F)   No service line shall be laid in any trench wherein is laid a gas
pipe, sewer pipe, or any other facility of a public service company or
municipality, unless authorized and approved by the borough.
   (G)   All injuries to service pipes or street mains caused by careless or
negligent work or improper filling or excavations for any purpose shall be
chargeable to the person causing such injury, and the expense of repairing the
same shall be recovered from such person.
   (H)   No owner or tenant of any premises supplied by the borough shall
furnish water to other persons or families except as specified in his or her
application and approved by the borough. In case of violation of this rule, the
supply shall be shut off and the applicant shall be charged with the proper
actual or estimated charge for such service, in accordance with the rates of
the borough.
   (I)   Meters will be maintained by the borough, so far as ordinary wear and
tear are concerned, but damage due to freezing where the meters are located
inside the building or cellar, or due to external causes under the applicant’s
control, shall be paid for by the applicant.
   (J)   The applicant shall notify the borough of any injury to or
nonperformance by the meter, as soon as it comes to his or her knowledge.
   (K)   Where water is furnished by meter, the quantity recorded by it shall
be conclusive on both the applicant and the borough, except when the meter has
been found to be registering inaccurately or has ceased to register. In the
latter case, the quantity may be determined by the average registration of the
meter when in order.
   (L)   In case of a disputed account involving the accuracy of a meter, such
meter shall be tested upon the request of the applicant. In the event that the
meter as tested is found to have an error in registration of 4% or more, the
bills will be increased or decreased accordingly.
(Prior Code, § 933.08) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926)
§ 51.08 APPLICATION REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   On or after January 1, 1982, a person, natural or otherwise, who
desires water service from the borough, shall make written application therefor
on an application form provided by the borough.
   (B)   The application shall be signed by the owner of the premises to be
serviced with water, where the owner is to be the occupier of the premises.
   (C)   The application shall be signed by the owner of the premises to be
serviced with water, where the premises are to be occupied by a tenant, and
when the water service statement is to be mailed to the owner.
   (D)   The application shall be signed by the owner of the premises to be
serviced with water and by the tenant, where the premises are to be occupied by
a tenant, and when the water service statement is to be mailed to the tenant.
In all such cases, the application shall be accompanied by a security deposit
for a sum established, from time to time, by resolution of Council.
   (E)   As used in this section, the singular shall include the plural, and
the plural shall include the singular.
(Prior Code, § 933.09) (Ord. 539, passed 11-23-1981)
§ 51.09 NEW METERS.
   (A)   New buildings requiring a new meter will be charged the actual cost of
the meter. The charge for reducing or enlarging an existing meter will be the
actual cost of the meter. Reducing and enlarging will be at the owner’s
request.
   (B)   All plumbing changes must be done by the owner and at the owner’s
cost. Request for a meter to be removed must be in writing and must be
accompanied by a nonrefundable fee as set from time to time by resolution of
Council to the borough. This request must also be approved by the Borough Code
Enforcement Officer.
(Prior Code, § 933.10) (Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003)
**** SERVICES AND RATES ****
§ 51.20 RESPONSIBILITY FOR CHARGES.
   (A)   All charges for water are to be assessed against the owner of the
property wherein the water is to be used.
   (B)   Responsibility for water rates imposed in connection with multiple
dwelling units serviced by one meter and/or receiving one statement for the
consumption of water shall be imposed upon the owner or owners of said building
or structure, and any such statement for subject water services shall be
appropriately forwarded to such owner or owners.
(Prior Code, § 931.01) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926; Ord. 557, passed 11-21-1985)
§ 51.21 USE OF METERS; LOCATION.
   The borough will sell water to consumers only through meters to be furnished
by the borough for that purpose, and at the rates established by §
51.22. The meter shall be set inside the building to be furnished with water,
wherever it is practicable to do so, in such position that no connection shall
be between it and the main line furnishing it with water.
(Prior Code, § 931.02) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926)
§ 51.22 IMPOSITION OF WATER RATES.
   Effective beginning the billing period November 15, 2016, the following
scheduled rates for the consumption of water are hereby imposed, which rates
will be sufficient to cover all operational and maintenance costs of the Water
Treatment Plant, the debt service on the PENNVEST loan, and all other
outstanding debt of the borough. The following rates shall apply to each
specific metered service connection to the main water distribution system
regardless of whether two or more service connections provide water to an
individual customer.
   (A)   Monthly minimum charges. Minimum monthly charges shall be as set from
time to time by resolution of Council.
   (B)   Monthly bulk consumption charges. Monthly bulk consumption charges
shall be as set from time to time by resolution of Council.
   (C)   Private fire hydrants.
      (1)   Direction connection to main each: shall be as set from time to
time by resolution of Council..
      (2)   The customer shall be entitled to the number of hydrants, hose
connections, or sprinkler heads indicated by the charge for the size of service
pipe connection employed from the main in the street, and all over this number
will be billed at the regular schedule of rates, except that where fire service
is permitted though the same service line and meter as providing normal water
service, the regular minimum charge shall apply as indicated for the size of
the meter employed.
      (3)   The customer will understand that a charge for private fire
protection is primarily a compensation for standing ready to serve and that the
amount of said charge does not contemplate the use of water, therefore,
excepting for test purposes and for actual extinguishing of fire, additional
charges will be rendered for any general use of water through such connections
and on the basis of actual delivery.
   (D)   Sprinkler or fire service system. Sprinkler or fire service system
charges shall be as set from time to time by resolution of Council.
   (E)   Touch read remote meters. All customers are required to install a
remote meter reading device on the outside of each dwelling and/or other unit
receiving water service. There will be a fee assessed against a customer in the
amount of one-third of the cost of a new meter and remote. Installation is to
be performed by an approved representative of the borough.
   (F)   Shut-off notice. Prior to shutting off any water service to a
customer, the borough shall give notice to the customer by certified mail and
by posting a shut-off notice on the house for a period of not less than three
days prior to shutting off service. The posted three-day shut-off notice is a
charge as set from time to time by resolution of Council.
   (G)   Additional charges.
      (1)   Work order.
         (a)   Generation. Generation of a work order to shut off utility
service for reason of non-payment: as set from time to time by resolution of
Council.
         (b)   Completion. Completion of a work order resulting in utility
service turned off for reason of nonpayment: as set from time to time by
resolution of Council.
      (2)   Final water meter reading. The final water meter reading: as set
from time to time by resolution of Council. (Snowbirds are exempt and customers
requiring a final meter reading because they are purchasing property with the
borough and moving into the “new service address.”)
      (3)   Magistrate letter. Sending a Magistrate letter: as set from time to
time by resolution of Council.
      (4)   Partial payments. Partial payments for utility service will not be
accepted.
(Prior Code, § 931.03) (Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003; Ord. 662, passed 12-29-
2005; Ord. 692, passed 12-29-2010; Ord. 696, passed 10-10-2011; Ord. 704,
passed 12-27-2012; Ord. 731, passed 7-2-2016)
§ 51.23 DISCONTINUANCE OF SERVICE; EMERGENCIES; LAWN SPRINKLING.
   (A)   Service under any application may be discontinued for any of the
following reasons:
      (1)   For failure to protect and keep the meter, connections, service
lines, or fixtures in good order;
      (2)   For neglecting to make or renew advance payment, or for nonpayment
of any account for water supplied, for water service, for fire protection, or
for meter or service maintenance, or of any scheduled fee or charge, within 30
days of the billing date;
      (3)   For molesting any service pipe, meter, curb stopcock, valve seal,
or other appliance of the borough controlling or regulating the water supply;
      (4)   In case of the vacancy of premises; or
      (5)   For violation of any rules of the borough.
   (B)   Water will be turned off of any premises, under the written order of
the applicant, without in any way affecting the existing application.
   (C)   No water fixtures or appliances in connection therewith will be
considered cut off until it is disconnected so that it cannot be used again, or
is sealed in a manner satisfactory to the borough.
   (D)   No plumber, owner, or other unauthorized person shall turn the water
on or off at any corporation stop or curb stop or disconnect or remove the
meter without the consent of the borough. Violators are subject to the
penalties imposed by the law and this subchapter.
   (E)   As necessity may arise in case of break, emergency, or for other
unavoidable cause, the borough shall have the right to temporarily cut off the
water supply in order to make necessary repairs, connections, and the like, but
the borough will use all reasonable and practicable measures to notify the
consumer of such discontinuance of service. In such case, the borough shall not
be liable for any damage or inconvenience suffered by the applicant or for any
claim against it at any time for interruption in service, reducing the supply,
inadequate pressure, or poor quality of the water, as a result of causes beyond
its control.
   (F)   The borough shall have the right to reserve a sufficient supply of
water at all times in its reservoirs to provide for fire and other emergencies
and it may restrict or regulate the quantity of water used by consumers in case
of scarcity or whenever the public welfare requires it. No street or lawn
sprinkling shall be done at a time of water shortage or other water emergency
declared by the Mayor. Sprinkling shall be discontinued whenever an alarm of
fire is sounded.
(Prior Code, § 931.04) (Ord. 159, passed 2-3-1926) Penalty, see §
51.99
§ 51.24 RENEWAL OF SERVICE.
   (A)   Conditions. Service will be renewed under a proper application when
conditions under which such service was discontinued are corrected and upon
payment of all charges and penalties provided in this subchapter.
   (B)   Turn-on and turn-off charge. In all cases where water from the
borough’s water system is turned on into a customer’s building, where the water
service is turned off, or where there is a change in customer billing, there
will be a charge in the amount as set from time to time by resolution of
Council.
(Prior Code, § 931.05) (Ord. 458, passed 8-19-1968; Ord. 518, passed 12-13-
1977; Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003)
§ 51.25 DISCONTINUANCE OF WATER SERVICE TO VIOLATORS OF SEWER RULES.
   (A)   It is the policy of the borough to discontinue utility service to
customers by reason of nonpayment of bills only after notice and a meaningful
opportunity to be heard on disputed bills. The borough’s form for application
for utility service and all bills shall contain, in addition to the title,
address, room number and telephone number of the official in charge of billing,
clearly visible and easily readable provisions to the effect:
      (1)   That all bills are due and payable on or before the date set forth
on the bill;
      (2)   That if any bill is not paid by or before that date, a second bill
will be mailed containing a cutoff notice that if the bill is not paid within
ten days of the mailing of the second bill, service will be discontinued for
nonpayment; and
      (3)   That any customer disputing the correctness of his or her bill
shall have a right to a hearing at which time he or she may be represented in
person and by counsel or any other person of his or her choosing and may
present orally or in writing his or her complaint and contentions to the
borough official in charge of utility billing. This official shall be
authorized to order that the customer’s service not be discontinued and shall
have the authority to make a final determination of the customer’s complaint.
   (B)   Requests for delays or waiver of payment will not be entertained; only
questions of proper and correct billing will be considered. In the absence of
payment of the bill rendered or resort to the hearing procedure provided
herein, service will be discontinued at the time specified, but in no event
until the charges have been due and unpaid for at least 30 days.
   (C)   When it becomes necessary for the borough to discontinue utility
service to a customer for nonpayment of bills, service will be reinstated only
after all bills for service then due have been paid, along with a turn-on
charge in the sum as set from time to time by resolution of Council.
§ 51.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.
CHAPTER 52: SEWER
Section

**** CONNECTIONS AND REGULATIONS ****
§ 52.01 CONNECTIONS REQUIRED.
   All land in the borough which adjoins or abuts on any street, avenue, or
alley in which a public sewer, whether sanitary, storm, or combined, has been
laid, or which is a part of a lot in one of the several plans of lots within
the borough, which lot adjoins or abuts upon any street, avenue, or alley in
which a public sewer, whether sanitary, storm, or combined, has been laid,
shall be connected with such sewer. The discharge of all fecal matter, human
excrement, kitchen and laundry wastes, and any other sewage from the premises
shall be made into a sanitary sewer, if one exists, and if not, into a combined
sewer. The discharge of all storm and rain water from roofs of dwellings and
commercial buildings, from drains or from any other source shall be made, by
suitable gutters and downspouts, into a storm sewer, where one exists, and if
not, into a combined sewer. Every such property shall be connected separately
and independently with the sewer through the house connection branch nearest
thereto in a downstream direction. For connections for sanitary wastes, the
grouping of buildings upon one house sewer shall not be permitted except under
special circumstances and for good sanitary reasons with special permission
granted by Council upon recommendation from the Superintendent of Public Works.
(Prior Code, § 923.01) (Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963)
§ 52.02 CONDITIONS PREREQUISITE TO CONNECTION.
   Except when a connection is made while a public sewer is being constructed
and before the excavation for the public sewer has been covered, no person
shall make or cause to be made any connection of his or her property with any
of the borough sewers until he or she has fulfilled all of the following
conditions.
   (A)   He or she shall notify the Superintendent of Public Works of his or
her desire and intention to make such connection at least 24 hours before the
time the connection is to be made, in order that the Superintendent of Public
Works or his or her authorized agent may be present to supervise the work of
the connection.
   (B)   He or she shall pay a sewer connection fee in an amount to be
determined by Council from time to time, payable to the Borough Secretary, for
the use of the borough.
   (C)   He or she shall apply for and obtain a permit to excavate in the
street in accordance with §§
150.15 through 150.45.
(Prior Code, § 923.02) (Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963)
§ 52.03 STANDARDS AND SPECIFICATIONS.
   All work of making connections to any of the borough sewers shall be done
under the personal supervision of the Superintendent of Public Works or his or
her authorized agent and shall conform to the requirements of this section. All
connections shall be made at the place where the “Y” in the borough sewer is
provided, but if no “Y” is provided in the sewer, then the property owner
making such connection shall, at his or her expense, install the “Y” making
such connection. All connections to the borough sewer shall be made by a master
plumber under the supervision of the Superintendent of Public Works or his or
her representative. All joints shall be sealed, made airtight, and made smooth
and clean inside, with all sewers in straight alignment and of proper grade, so
as to provide free flow of sewage matter without any obstructions. In addition,
joints shall be made in conformity with the borough’s specification for its
sanitary sewers. All work pertaining to the connection with the borough’s
sewers shall be, financially and otherwise, the responsibility of the owner of
the property with which connection is made, subject to the right of supervision
hereby reserved by the borough.
(Prior Code, § 923.03) (Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.04 PROHIBITED WASTE.
   No person shall connect or cause to be connected with any of the public
sewers in the borough, directly or indirectly, any steam exhaust boiler blow-
off, sediment drip, or any pipe carrying or constructed to carry hot water or
acid, germicide, grease, brewery mash, gasoline, naptha, benzine, oil, or any
other substance detrimental to the sewers or to the operation of the sewerage
system or the sewage disposal works of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 923.04) (Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.05 PRIVY VAULTS, CESSPOOLS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   (1)   It is hereby required that all privy vaults or cesspools or
similar receptacles for human excrement on all occupied premises within the
borough which are accessible to the public sewer system shall be cleansed and
filled within 30 days after notice to do so has been served upon the owner of
the premises on which such privy vault or cesspool is located, to the end that
they shall be altogether abandoned.
      (2)   No such owner shall fail to comply with this requirement.
   (B)   Such cleansing and filling shall be done under the direction and
supervision of the Health Officer of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 923.05) (Ord. 134, passed 1-9-1924; Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963)
Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.06 BOROUGH’S REMEDY FOR NONPERFORMANCE OF PRIVY VAULTS.
   Upon failure or neglect of the owner of occupied premises on which there is
a privy vault or cesspool to cleanse and fill the same in accordance with §
52.05, the borough shall cleanse and fill such privy vault or cesspool or cause
the same to be cleansed and filled and collect the cost and expense thereof,
with a penalty of 10% additional for such failure or neglect, from the owner of
the property on which such work was done.
(Prior Code, § 923.06) (Ord. 134, passed 1-9-1924)
§ 52.07 CONNECTION OF PRIVY VAULTS AND THE LIKE TO SEWERS PROHIBITED.
   No privy vault, cesspool, or similar receptacle for human excrement shall at
any time be connected with any of the borough sewers.
(Prior Code, § 923.07) (Ord. 421, passed 3-15-1963) Penalty, see §
52.99
**** SERVICE AND RATES ****
§ 52.20 SERVICE AND RATES.
   The sewer system of the borough is under control of the Borough of Ford City
Sewage Authority. Current rates and charges are on file with the Authority.
§ 52.21 RULES AND REGULATIONS.
   The borough reserves the right to, and may from time to time, adopt, revise,
amend, and re-adopt such rules and regulations as it deems necessary and proper
for the use and operation of the sewer system and all such rules and
regulations shall be and become a part of this subchapter and enforceable as
part of this subchapter. Such rules and regulations, when adopted by Council,
shall be printed in book or pamphlet form and published at length according to
law, and when so published shall be and become a part of this subchapter and
enforceable as a part of this subchapter.   
(Prior Code, § 921.10) (Ord. 420, passed 3-15-1963; Ord. 699, passed 12-12-
2011)
§ 52.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.

**** SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT ****
§ 53.01 SHORT TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known as the “Borough of Ford City Solid Waste
Ordinance.”
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023)
§ 53.02 PURPOSE.
   (A)   It is the intent and purpose of this subchapter to promote the public
health, safety, and welfare and to eliminate public health hazards,
environmental pollution, and economic loss by providing that all residential,
commercial, industrial, and institutional municipal solid waste accumulated or
stored upon any property within the borough shall be collected, transported,
and disposed of within the following conditions.
      (1)   All residential properties shall be served by the borough’s
contracted hauler.
      (2)   All nonresidential properties not subject to the borough’s contract
shall have contracted waste service with a hauler.
      (3)   All municipal solid waste shall be disposed of at an approved
facility, in accordance with the County Solid Waste Management Plan, as well as
local, state, federal, and county laws and ordinances.
   (B)   This subchapter is enacted to assure borough compliance with the
Municipal Waste Act, being 53 P.S. §§ 4000.101 et seq.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023)
§ 53.03 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACT 97. The Solid Waste Management Act, 35 P.S. §§ 6018.101 et seq., as
amended.
   ACT 101. The Municipal Waste Planning, Recycling, and Waste Reduction Act of
1988, 53 P.S. §§ 4000.101 et seq., as amended.
   AGREEMENT. The contract between the borough and contractor for the
collection and disposal of municipal solid waste and/or the collection and
processing of recyclable materials.
   BOROUGH. The Borough of Ford City.
   BULKY WASTE. Large items of solid waste, including, but not limited to,
furniture and large auto parts, which may require special handling due to their
size, shape, or weight, but not including garbage, rubbish, construction
demolition waste, dead animals, electronic waste, hazardous waste, white waste,
stable matter, or yard waste.
   COMMERCIAL ESTABLISHMENT. Properties used primarily for commercial,
religious, or industrial purposes.
   CONSTRUCTION DEMOLITION WASTE. All municipal and residential waste,
including without limitation building materials, grubbing waste, and rubble
resulting from construction, remodeling, repair, and demolition operations on
houses, commercial buildings, and other structures and pavements.
   CONTAINER. Containers provided or designated by the borough for the curbside
collection of municipal solid waste.
   CONTRACTED HAULER or HAULER. The person, corporation, or partnership
performing municipal solid waste collection and disposal and/or recyclable
materials collection and processing under contract with the borough.
   CURBSIDE COLLECTION. The collection of municipal solid waste and/or
recyclable materials from one containers and/or recycling containers placed at
the alley or curb which would be unobstructed and clearly visible to the
contractor. Collection from containers and/or recyclable containers more than
five feet from the curb is not included in this bid, nor is collection from
containers and/or recycling containers within five-foot areas that are hidden
or otherwise obstructed from view of the contractor. In areas where there is no
curb, CURBSIDE COLLECTION refers to the edge of the traveled roadway, so long
as the placement of containers and/or recycling containers does not impede
vehicular or pedestrian traffic or create hazards to vehicles or persons
traveling in this area.
   DEAD ANIMALS. Animals or portions thereof equal to or greater than ten
pounds in weight that have expired from any cause, except those slaughtered or
killed for human use.
   DISPOSAL SITE. A refuse depository, including, but not limited to, municipal
solid waste landfills, transfer stations, incinerators, and waste processing/
separation centers with all permits required by the laws of the federal
government and commonwealth for the processing or final disposal of all refuse
properly discarded under the state laws to receive for processing final
disposal of refuse and dead animals.
   ELECTRONIC WASTE or E-WASTE. All discarded electrical or electronic devices,
including without limitation computers, office electronic equipment,
entertainment device electronics, mobile phones, and television sets.
   GARBAGE. Any solid waste derived from animal, grain, fruit, or vegetable
matter that is capable of being decomposed by microorganisms with sufficient
rapidity to cause such nuisances as odors, gases, or vectors. GARBAGE shall not
include bulky waste, construction demolition waste, dead animals, rubbish,
electronic waste, hazardous waste, white waste, stable matter, or yard waste.
   HAZARDOUS WASTE. Any solid waste or combination of solid wastes, as defined
in Act 97, which because of its quantity, concentration, or physical, chemical,
or infectious characteristics, may:
      (1)   Cause or significantly contribute to an increase in mortality or an
increase in morbidity in either an individual or the total population; or
      (2)   Pose a substantial present or potential hazard to human health or
the environment when improperly treated, stored, transported, disposed of, or
otherwise managed.
   INSTITUTIONAL ESTABLISHMENT. Educational facilities, including, but not
limited to, schools and universities.
   MUNICIPAL ESTABLISHMENT. Public facilities operated by a municipality, a
municipal authority, or other governmental or quasi-governmental authority or
agency.
   MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE. Garbage, rubbish, residential refuse, lunchroom or
office waste, and other material, including solid, liquid, semisolid, or
contained gaseous material resulting from operation of residential units,
municipal establishments, commercial establishments, institutional
establishment, and community activities, and any sludge not meeting the
definition of residual or hazardous waste under Act 97 from a municipal,
commercial or institutional water supply treatment plant, wastewater treatment
plant, or air pollution control facility. This term does not include bulky
waste, recyclable materials, construction demolition waste, dead animals,
electronic waste, hazardous waste, white waste, stable matter, or yard waste.
   PRODUCER. An occupant of a residential unit who generates residential
refuse, commercial establishments, and institutional establishments.
   RECYCLABLE MATERIALS. Materials generated within the borough that can be
separated from municipal solid waste and returned to commerce to be reused as a
resource in the development of useful products, including without limitation:
clear class; colored glass; aluminum, steel, ferrous and bi-metal containers;
paper; and clear plastic (PET) and high density polyethylene (HDPE) plastic
containers, including milk, water, juice, and soda bottles.
   RECYCLING CONTAINERS. Containers provided or designated by the borough for
the curbside collection of recyclable materials.
   RESIDENTIAL REFUSE. All garbage and rubbish generated by a producer at a
residential unit.
   RESIDENTIAL UNIT. A dwelling within the corporate limits of the borough
occupied by a person or group of persons. A RESIDENTIAL UNIT shall be deemed
occupied when either water or domestic light and power services are being
supplied thereto. A condominium dwelling, whether of single or multilevel
construction, consisting of four or less contiguous or separate single-family
dwelling units, shall be treated as a RESIDENTIAL UNIT, except that each
single-family dwelling within any such RESIDENTIAL UNIT shall be billed
separately as a RESIDENTIAL UNIT.
   RUBBISH. All non-putrescible municipal solid waste except garbage and other
decomposable matter. This category includes, but is not limited to, waste wood,
wood products, tree trimmings, grass cuttings, dead plants, weeds, leaves, dead
trees or branches thereof, chips, shavings, sawdust, printed matter,
pasteboard, rags, straw, used clothing, used and discarded shoes and boots,
combustible waste pulp and other products such as are used for packaging or
wrapping, crockery and glass, ashes, cinders, floor sweepings, mineral or
metallic substances, and any and all other waste materials. RUBBISH shall not
include bulky waste, construction demolition waste, dead animals, electronic
waste, garbage, hazardous waste, white waste, stable matter, or yard waste.
   STABLE MATTER. All manure and other waste matter normally accumulated and
resulting from the keeping of animals such as, but not limited to, dogs and
cats.
   WHITE WASTE. All discarded appliances, including without limitation
refrigerators and water heaters.
   YARD WASTE. Leaves, grass clippings, garden residue, tree trimmings, chipped
shrubbery, and other vegetative material commonly found in or about the yard.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023)
§ 53.04 COLLECTION OF MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person other than persons defined herein to
collect and/or transport municipal solid waste which is generated within the
borough as a regular hauling business. The borough may decide to enter into an
exclusive contract for residential and/or commercial refuse collection or any
part thereof.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.05 CONTAINERS GENERALLY.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of every person owning a residential, commercial,
industrial, or institutional property within the borough where municipal solid
waste is generated and accumulated, by his/her own expense and cost, except as
otherwise specified in this subchapter, to provide and keep at all times a
sufficient number of containers to hold all municipal solid waste accumulated
between intervals of collection of such waste by a hauler; and to ensure the
sanitary and legal disposal of such waste in accordance with this subchapter
and all other borough, state, and federal applicable laws and regulations.
   (B)   It shall be the responsibility of each every person owning a
residential, commercial, industrial, or institutional property within the
borough to keep all refuse receptacles in good repair and sanitary condition.
Any receptacle which does not conform to the provisions of this subchapter or
that may have ragged or sharp edges or any other defect liable to hamper or
injure the hauler shall be promptly replaced upon notice. Failure to comply
within ten days will result in the removal of the defective receptacle as
refuse.
   (C)   No container shall be so filled that the lid will not fit tightly.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.06 STORAGE ON RESIDENTIAL PROPERTIES.
   (A)   Containers. All municipal solid waste accumulated by owners of
residential properties and/or the occupants of residential properties shall be
placed in containers for collection by a hauler. Containers used for curbside
collection shall be watertight, covered plastic or metallic cans that can be
easily and quickly handled by one person. Refuse containers shall be not less
than five gallons nor more than 65 gallons in capacity. The weight of a single
filled container shall not exceed 50 pounds.
   (B)   Location of containers. For residential properties with curbside
collection, each container shall be placed for collection so as to be
accessible to the hauler at ground level and at a point immediately behind the
curbline of the street, within no more than ten feet of the cartway of the
street or alley from which the collection with a vehicle is made if there is no
curbing, or at a location mutually agreeable between the resident, the borough,
and the hauler. Failure to place containers at such locations may result in
refuse not being collected.
   (C)   Removal of containers after collection. All containers shall be
removed from the curb or street within 24 hours after collection. Failure to
remove containers from the curb or street in accordance with this section shall
constitute a violation of this subchapter, subject to the penalties contained
herein.
   (D)   Disabilities. Persons with physical disabilities (as defined in the
Americans with Disabilities Act, being 42 U.S.C. §§ 12101 et seq.) whose
residence is included under the borough’s hauler contract may place refuse at
other-than-street or alley locations at no additional charge when approved by
the borough and its hauler.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.07 STORAGE ON COMMERCIAL, INDUSTRIAL, AND INSTITUTIONAL PROPERTIES.
   (A)   Containers. Storage of municipal solid waste on commercial,
industrial, and institutional properties shall be done in a dumpster for
collection by a hauler. If the amount of waste generated per week is less than
a two-yard dumpster the same type of containers as required for residential
properties as defined in § 51.19 shall be allowed if agreed upon by the
borough, customer, and the hauler.
   (B)   Location of containers. Dumpsters for the storage and collection of
municipal solid waste at commercial, industrial, or institutional properties
shall be placed, whenever possible, at ground level and at a point on the
property being serviced by the hauler which will enable clear and easy access
to the dumpster by the hauler’s vehicle. This location, if required, shall be
in compliance with the borough’s zoning ordinance or other applicable
regulations for the location of such containers. Such locations shall not
interfere with travel upon public or private sidewalks, walkways, driveways,
roads, streets, highways, alleys, or entrances and exits of public or private
buildings.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.08 TRANSPORTATION AND DISPOSAL OF MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE.
   (A)   Any person transporting municipal solid waste within the borough shall
prevent or remedy any spillage from vehicles or containers used in the
transport of such municipal solid waste. The haulers shall collect municipal
solid waste in vehicles which are suitable for such collection, which are
dedicated for use in performance of such collection, and which bear prominent
legible marking, signs, or decals identifying them as being municipal solid
waste collection vehicles, and stating the name of the hauler. The vehicles
shall be securely covered, watertight, strongly built, and kept thoroughly
cleaned and well maintained.
   (B)   The borough, or its designated agent, shall have the authority to
inspect the vehicles used for the transport of municipal solid waste as it
deems necessary to determine compliance with this subchapter. The hauler shall
correct deficiencies immediately upon notification by the borough or its
designated agent, and said vehicle shall not be used for refuse collection
until the deficiencies have been corrected.
   (C)   All municipal solid waste produced, collected, and transported from
within the jurisdictional limits of the borough shall be disposed of at the
facilities designated in the County Solid Waste Management Plan.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.09 UNLAWFUL DISPOSITION OF MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to bring any municipal solid waste into
the borough or to transport municipal solid waste from one address to another
within or outside of the borough for the purpose of taking advantage of the
collection service to avoid the cost of collection.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.10 BULK WASTE.
   (A)   Bulk waste shall be disposed of in accordance with the Armstrong
County Solid Waste Management Plan at a permitted disposal facility, a facility
designated by the borough to take such bulk items, or a legitimate salvage
dealer that is in the business of disposing or recycling such items. Bulk waste
may be transported in a vehicle appropriate to the type of waste so as to
prevent spillage, accidental loss, and the like.
   (B)   Nothing contained herein shall be deemed to prohibit any person not
regularly engaged in the business of collecting municipal solid waste from
hauling his or her bulk waste to a permitted disposal facility or to a disposal
facility as designated by the borough in accordance with the regulations of the
disposal facility.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.11 CONSTRUCTION AND DEMOLITION WASTE.
   All waste materials resulting from the building, structural alteration,
repair, construction, or demolition of buildings or structures shall be
disposed of as permitted by applicable borough, county, state, and federal laws
and regulations as may be in effect or as may be subsequently imposed. It shall
be the responsibility of the property owner to ensure the disposal of such
waste in accordance with applicable laws and regulations. Nothing contained
herein shall be deemed to prohibit any person not regularly engaged in the
business of collecting municipal solid waste from hauling his or her
construction and demolition waste to a state permitted disposal facility or to
a disposal facility as designated by the borough in accordance with the
regulations of the disposal facility.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.12 ACCUMULATION OF WASTE PROHIBITED.
   For reasons of health and sanitation, it shall be unlawful for any person to
accumulate or permit to accumulate upon private property in the borough, any of
the types of wastes defined under §
53.03.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.13 SERVICE FEE AND CHARGES.
   (A)   It shall be the duty and responsibility of every owner of property and
every person occupying a dwelling unit, premises or place of business within
the borough where municipal solid waste is generated to pay a service fee for
the collection and disposal of this waste, except as otherwise provided by this
subchapter, to a hauler, or to the borough. A hauler or the borough may
discontinue service for nonpayment of service fees by a residential,
commercial, industrial, or institutional establishment. To discontinue
residential service, the customer shall be in arrears a minimum of 90 days and
shall have received during this period a minimum of two written notices from
the borough regarding the amount due and procedures for payment. The second
notice shall be sent by certified mail. At the time of the discontinuance of
service, the customer shall be considered in violation of this subchapter.
Discontinuance of service due to nonpayment of service fees shall not relieve
the resident or establishment from abiding by all of the requirements of this
subchapter. The borough, or its designated agent, hauler, or exclusive
contractor may impose an administrative fee to reinstate customers whose
service is disconnected.
   (B)   The cost of municipal solid waste collection service, and the
responsibility of payment therefore, shall be borne by the owner of the
premises from which said municipal solid waste is collected or upon which
premises said municipal solid waste is generated and disposed of. No agreement
between an owner and occupant or other person shall relieve the owner of any
premises from liability for payments as set forth hereinafter.
   (C)   All service fees established for the collection of municipal solid
waste by the borough shall be established by a resolution of Borough Council.
Service charges so established shall be reviewed at least annually and adjusted
as necessary to ensure that all costs involved in the collection and disposal
of municipal solid waste, including the administrative costs of the borough, or
its designated agent, are covered by the service charges.
(Ord. 757, passed 12-11-2023)
**** MUNICIPAL GARBAGE COLLECTION SERVICE ****
§ 53.25 FINDING.
   The Council of the borough hereby makes a finding that all residential water
service customers of the borough are users of the garbage collection service
provided by the borough, and vice-versa.
(Prior Code, § 953.01) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981)
§ 53.26 USE OF SERVICE REQUIRED.
   (A)   All residents and businesses of the borough must use the garbage
collection service provided by the borough to collect and dispose of garbage
originating from their residences or places of business.
   (B)   The borough shall be the only garbage collection service in and for
the borough unless it grants a permit or approves a contract to other garbage
collection services.
   (C)   No person, resident, or business may collect garbage in the borough
without a borough permit or approved contract.
(Prior Code, § 953.02) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981; Ord. 591, passed 6-20-
1989) Penalty, see §
53.99
§ 53.27 WRITTEN APPLICATION REQUIRED.
   After the effective date of this subchapter, all new residents or residents
who change their place of residence shall make written application for garbage
collection service on application forms provided by the borough.
(Prior Code, § 953.03) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981)
§ 53.28 APPLICATION SIGNATURES.
   (A)   The application form shall be signed by the owners of the residential
premises to be serviced, where the owner is to be the occupier of the
residential premises.
   (B)   The application form shall be signed by the owner of the residential
premises to be serviced, where the residential premises are to be occupied by a
tenant and when the garbage collection service statement is to be mailed to the
owner.
   (C)   The application form shall be signed by the owner of the residential
premises to be serviced and by the tenant, where the residential premises are
to be occupied by a tenant, and when the garbage collection service statement
is to be mailed to the tenant.
(Prior Code, § 953.04) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981)
§ 53.29 USAGE.
   As used in this subchapter, the singular shall include the plural.
(Prior Code, § 953.05) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981)
§ 53.30 MONTHLY GARBAGE COLLECTION FEE.
   (A)   Fee levied.
      (1)   There is hereby established and levied upon all users of the
garbage collection service provided by the borough a monthly garbage collection
fee as established by resolution of the Borough Council from time to time and
monthly landfill closure cost fee as established by resolution of the Borough
Council from time to time, effective on passage.
      (2)   This division (A) shall become effective on the billing cycle due
January 1, 2011.
   (B)   Applicability. Effective February 17, 1985, this section and the
monthly fee shall apply to each individual resident or residents maintaining a
separate dwelling unit, apartment, or individual unit consisting of a separate
customer.
   (C)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      APARTMENT. Each division of any dwelling house or building, whether
single or double, that is occupied by different families or family units or
tenants as a separate dwelling unit.
      CUSTOMER. Any individual person or family group or any commercial,
business, professional, government or fraternal organization occupying a
dwelling unit or other unit receiving water or sewage service.
      DWELLING UNIT. Any building or division of such building or apartment in
such building which is used for human habitation by a separate family group or
by separate individuals.
   (D)   Pickup limitations.
      (1)   Each customer for garbage collection service as defined in division
(C) above shall be limited to four bags or items of garbage per pick-up.
Additional bags or items of garbage in excess of four will be picked up at each
collection only if the bags have visibly attached garbage tags which may be
purchased from the borough.
      (2)   No bag or item of garbage shall be picked up at each collection if
it weighs in excess of what one person can lift.
(Prior Code, § 953.06) (Ord. 541, passed 12-28-1981; Ord. 546, passed 5-9-1983;
Ord. 555, passed 1-17-1985; Ord. 576, passed 12-15-1987; Ord. 580, passed 5-17-
1988; Ord. 587, passed 1-4-1989; Ord. 597, passed 5-15-1990; Ord. 602, passed
12-7-1990; Ord. 613, passed 12-30-1991; Ord. 641, passed 1-15-2002; Ord. 649,
passed 12-9-2002; Ord. 654, passed 11- -2003; Ord. 662, passed 12-29-2005; Ord.
676, passed 5-19-2008; Ord. 693, passed 12-29-2010)
§ 53.31 ANNUAL TRASH DAY.
   The borough shall conduct an annual trash day each year. Rules governing the
collection process shall be promulgated from time to time by Borough Council
regulation.
(Prior Code, § 953.08) (Ord. 676, passed 5-19-2008)
§ 53.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.

CHAPTER 70: GENERAL TRAFFIC REGULATIONS
Section
General Provisions
   
70.01   Definitions
Enforcement
   
70.15   Responsibility of Code Enforcement Officer, police, and firefighters

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 70.01 DEFINITIONS.
   The terms used in this Traffic Code shall have the meanings respectively
ascribed to them in the Vehicle Code of Pennsylvania, being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101
et seq., P.L. 162, § 1, as amended. In addition thereto, until otherwise
defined by Acts of Assembly, the following terms shall be defined as set forth
in this section.
   AUTHORIZED EMERGENCY VEHICLES. Vehicles of the Police Department or
Volunteer Fire Department and such ambulances and emergency vehicles of
municipal departments or public service corporations as are designated or
authorized by the Chief of Police.
   CURB. The boundaries of a street.
   DRIVER. Means and includes the rider or driver of animals, the rider of
bicycles, and the operator of vehicles.
   OFFICIAL TRAFFIC SIGN. All signs, markings, and lines not inconsistent with
this Traffic Code, painted, placed, or erected by authority of a public body or
officials having jurisdiction for the purpose of guiding, directing, warning,
or regulating traffic.   
   PARKED VEHICLE. A waiting vehicle and waiting vehicles drawn up alongside of
one another not parallel to the curb.   
   POLICE OFFICERS. Every officer or member of the Police Department, or any
officer authorized to regulate or direct traffic or to make arrests for
violations of traffic regulations.
   SIDEWALK. The portion of a street, alley, or road between the curb lines and
the adjacent property lines.
   STREET. The part of a public highway intended for vehicles.
   VEHICLE CODE or VEHICLE CODE OF PENNSYLVANIA. The Act of Assembly of April
29, 1959, P.L. No. 58, as amended.
(Prior Code, § 501.01) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917; Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
**** ENFORCEMENT ****
§ 70.15 RESPONSIBILITY OF CODE ENFORCEMENT OFFICER, POLICE, AND FIREFIGHTERS.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the Code Enforcement Officer and police
officers to enforce the provisions of this Traffic Code.
   (B)   Police officers are hereby authorized to direct all traffic either in
person or by means of visible or audible signals in conformity with the
provisions of this Traffic Code, provided, that in the event of a fire or other
emergency or to expedite traffic or safeguard pedestrians, police officers and
the officers and police patrol of the Volunteer Fire Department may direct
traffic, as conditions may require, notwithstanding the provisions of this
Traffic Code.
(Prior Code, § 503.01) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.16 TEMPORARY REGULATIONS.
   The Code Enforcement Officer is hereby authorized to make and enforce any
temporary regulations that may be necessary to make effective the provisions of
this Traffic Code under special conditions or in cases of emergency.
(Prior Code, § 503.02) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.17 OBEDIENCE TO POLICE OFFICERS.
   No person shall refuse or otherwise fail to comply with any lawful order,
signal, or direction of a police officer.
(Prior Code, § 503.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.18 PERSONS AFFECTED; COMPLIANCE REQUIRED.
   The provisions of this Traffic Code shall apply to public employees as well
as to private individuals, and no operator of any vehicle shall violate any of
the provisions of this Traffic Code, except as otherwise permitted herein.
(Prior Code, § 503.04) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.19 EMERGENCY VEHICLES.
   The provisions of this Traffic Code regulating the movement, parking, and
standing of vehicles shall not apply to authorized emergency vehicles while the
driver of such vehicle is operating the same in an emergency in the necessary
performance of public duties. The exemption shall not, however, protect the
driver of any such vehicle for the consequences of a disregard of the safety of
others.
(Prior Code, § 503.05) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.20 APPLICATION TO PERSONS RIDING, DRIVING ANIMALS, PUSHCARTS, BICYCLES.
   Every person propelling any pushcart or riding a bicycle or an animal upon a
roadway, and every person driving any animal shall be subject to the provisions
of this Traffic Code the same as the operator of any vehicle, except those
provisions which by their very nature can have no application.
(Prior Code, § 503.06) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.21 PROCEDURE UPON ARREST.
   Whenever any person is arrested for a violation of any of the provisions of
this Traffic Code, such person may be tried summarily, or the procedure shall
be as provided by the Vehicle Code of the state, being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101 et
seq.
(Prior Code, § 503.07) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.22 DISPOSITION AND USE OF FINES.
   All fines and penalties collected under the provisions of this Traffic Code,
being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101 et seq., except for prosecutions instituted under the
Vehicle Code of the state, shall be paid into the Borough Treasury and, unless
otherwise directed by state law, shall be used for the construction, repair,
and maintenance of the streets and highways of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 503.08) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 70.99 PENALTY FOR TITLE VII.
   Whoever violates any of the provisions of this Traffic Code, or any rules or
regulations made pursuant thereto, shall be fined not more than $50 or as may
be stipulated by the Vehicle Code of the state, being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101 et
seq., and, in default of the payment thereof, may be imprisoned in the County
Jail for not more than ten days.
(Prior Code, § 503.99)

**** STREET OBSTRUCTIONS AND SPECIAL USES ****
§ 71.01 INTERFERENCE WITH USE OF PUBLIC WAYS.
   The public streets and highways of the borough are hereby declared to be for
the common use of the traveling public except insofar as the same may be
restricted herein or by other proper municipal action. No person shall place or
cause to be placed any obstruction above, upon, or under any public street or
highway, except with the consent of Council and subject to such conditions as
Council may impose, and then at the risk and responsibility of such person.
(Prior Code, § 511.01) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.02 DEPOSITING GLASS, REFUSE, AND THE LIKE UPON PUBLIC WAYS.
   No person shall place or throw, or cause or permit to be placed or thrown,
any glass, metal, refuse, or other substances or materials upon any street or
highway.
(Prior Code, § 511.02) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.03 THROUGH STREETS.
   In the interest of public safety and convenience, Council shall from time to
time, by resolution, establish or abolish through streets or highways, and when
so established, the following additional regulations shall apply.
   (A)   Every operator of a vehicle or conveyance traveling upon any street or
highway intersecting any through street or highway thus designated shall bring
such vehicle or other conveyance to a full stop at the place where such street
or highway intersects, subject, however, to the direction of any traffic
control sign or signal or any police officer at such intersection.
   (B)   The operator of any vehicle who has come to a full stop as required
above, upon entering the through street or highway, as well as the operators of
vehicles on such through streets or highways, shall be subject to the usual
right-of-way rules prescribed by law and applicable to vehicles at
intersections.
   (C)   Council may, by resolution from time to time, direct the placing and
maintaining at any street or highway intersecting a through street or highway,
at or near the property line of the through street or highway such appropriate
signs, devices, or marks in the roadway bearing the words “thru traffic stop”
in letters at least six inches in height. All such signs shall be illuminated
at night or so placed as to be illuminated by street lights or by the
headlights of approaching motor vehicles.
(Prior Code, § 511.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.04 PLAY STREETS.
   Council may, by resolution from time to time, designate any street or
highway or part of a street or highway under its jurisdiction a “play street”
or “play highway” and may close such designated street or highway to general
traffic where interference to traffic will not be serious. Such streets or
highways, or portions of streets or highways, shall be used for play purposes
and shall be clearly designated by appropriate signs, specifying the hours
between which such streets or highways shall be closed to general traffic.   
(Prior Code, § 511.05) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 71.05 TEMPORARY USE RESTRICTIONS.
   Council may, by resolution from time to time, prohibit the operation of
vehicles upon any street or highway within its jurisdiction, or impose
restrictions as to the kinds of vehicles and the weight of vehicles and their
loads, for a total period not to exceed 90 days in any one calendar year,
whenever any such street or highway, by reason of improvement or repairs,
deterioration, rain, snow, or other climatic conditions, will be seriously
damaged or destroyed unless the use of vehicles thereon is prohibited or the
permissible weights reduced. However, appropriate signs shall be erected and
maintained designating the provisions of the ordinance, resolution, or rule, at
each end of that portion of any street or highway affected thereby.
(Prior Code, § 511.06) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 71.06 TOY VEHICLES UPON ROADWAY.
   No person upon roller skates or riding in or by means of any coaster, toy
vehicle, or similar device shall go upon any roadway except while crossing a
street on a crosswalk.
(Prior Code, § 511.07) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
71.99
**** TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICES ****
§ 71.20 PLACEMENT, PURCHASE, AND MAINTENANCE.
   Council shall, by resolution, determine and designate the character or type
of all traffic signs and signals and purchase the same when necessary, and all
signs and signals required hereunder for a particular purpose so far as
practicable shall be uniform as to type and location throughout the borough.
Subject to this provision, it shall be the duty of the Public Works Director,
Borough Manager and/or their designee, and he or she is hereby authorized and
required to, place and maintain or cause to be placed and maintained all
traffic signs and signals herein required.
(Prior Code, § 513.01) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
§ 71.21 COMPLIANCE; DRIVING TO RIGHT OF SIGNS.
   (A)   No operator shall disobey the instructions of any official traffic
sign or signal placed in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter
unless otherwise directed by a police officer.   
   (B)   Whenever and wherever there is placed in or upon the streets, alleys,
and highways of the borough, any monument, sign, post, dummy, or other marker,
indicating or directing that the users of such public way, when traveling
thereon, or in making any turn into or upon an intersecting highway, shall keep
to the right of such monument, sign, post dummy, or other marker, it shall be
the duty of the user of such highway to observe such directions, and it shall
be unlawful for any user of such highways to pass any such marker on the left
hand side thereof.
(Prior Code, § 513.02) (Ord. 191, passed 3-5-1935; Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.22 CROSSWALKS; TURNING AT INTERSECTIONS.
   Council may, by resolution from time to time, establish, designate, and
maintain or cause to be maintained by appropriate devices, marks or lines upon
the surface of the roadway, crosswalks, or intersections where there is
particular danger to pedestrians crossing the roadway, and may, by resolution
from time to time, regulate or prohibit turning at intersections and cause
proper signs to be erected and maintained giving notice of such regulations. It
shall be the duty of the Code Enforcement Officer to enforce such regulations
and prohibitions.
(Prior Code, § 513.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
**** INTERFERENCE WITH SNOW REMOVAL ****
§ 71.35 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   INDIVIDUAL. Any person, persons, partnership, firm, company, corporation or
other association.
   NOTICE. For purposes of authorizing the removing of a vehicle by an
authorized agent of the Borough, shall mean the placing by a borough employee
of a written notice upon the vehicle.
   NUISANCE. For purposes of this subchapter, shall include, but is not limited
to, the parking or leaving standing any vehicle upon any street of the borough
in such a way or at a time so as to prevent or interfere with the removal or
plowing of snow, the piling of snow upon the paved portion of the streets of
said borough, and the failure to remove snow and sleet from the sidewalks of
said borough.
   STREET. Includes alleys, avenues, boulevards, lanes, roads, streets, and
other public ways in the borough.
(Prior Code, § 515.01) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997)
§ 71.36 MANNER OF PARKING PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful for any individual to park or leave standing any
vehicle upon any street of the borough in such a way or at such a time that
will prevent or interfere with the removal or plowing of snow.
(Prior Code, § 515.02) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.37 NOTICE; FAILURE TO REMOVE.
   Any vehicle upon which proper notice has been placed and which has not been
removed by the owner thereof within 12 hours of said posting of notice may be
removed by authorized agent of the borough, and all charges incurred in such
removal, including, but not limited to, towing of said vehicle and storage
thereof, shall be paid by the owner of said vehicle. Said vehicle shall be
deemed a safety hazard under Act No. 81, Motor Vehicle Code of the state, §
3352, subsections (a) and (b), being 75 Pa.C.S. § 3352(a) and (b).
(Prior Code, § 515.03) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997)
§ 71.38 DUTY OF OWNER OR OCCUPANT.
   No owner or occupant of any lot or part of a lot within the borough shall
fail, within six hours after any fall or deposit of snow and sleet, to remove
the same from the pavements and sidewalks fronting the premises respectively
owned or occupied by him or her.
(Prior Code, § 515.04) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.39 PLACEMENT OF SNOW ON STREETS PROHIBITED.
   The streets of the borough are hereby declared to be for the common use of
the traveling public. No individual shall shovel, place, throw, or by the use
of any other means cause snow to be piled upon the paved portion of the streets
of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 515.05) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997) Penalty, see §
71.99
§ 71.40 EFFECTIVE DATES.
   This subchapter shall be effective from November 15 to the following April
30, inclusive, of each calendar year.
(Prior Code, § 515.06) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997)
§ 71.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
70.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any individual violating the provisions of §§
71.35 through
71.40, or aiding, abetting, or assisting therein, shall, upon conviction
thereof, before any District Magistrate in the county, be subject to a fine of
not less than $25 nor more than $600, together with costs of prosecution, which
fine shall be paid to the Borough Treasurer for the use of the borough, and in
default of payment of said fine and costs, shall be sentenced to undergo
imprisonment in the County Jail for not less than one day nor more than ten
days. Each day a vehicle shall remain parked or left standing in violation of
§§
71.35 through
71.40 shall constitute a separate violation.
(Prior Code, § 515.99) (Ord. 633, passed 3-3-1997)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 72.01 SPEED REGULATIONS.
   No person shall operate a vehicle upon the streets or highways of the
borough at a speed greater than that established by the Vehicle Code of the
state, being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101 et seq.
(Prior Code, § 523.01) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.02 MOTORCYCLES.
   No operator of any motorcycle shall carry any other person upon the
handlebars, frame, or tank of such vehicle, and no person shall so ride upon
any such vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 531.01) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
**** OPERATION GENERALLY ****
§ 72.15 U-TURNS.
   No operator of any vehicle shall make a U-turn or turn such vehicle as to
proceed in the opposite direction on the avenues, streets, or alleys of the
borough.
(Prior Code, § 521.01) (Ord. 477, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.16 BACKING.
   (A)   No operator of a vehicle shall back the same unless such movement can
be made in safety.
   (B)   No operator shall back a vehicle to make a turn if it obstructs
traffic, but shall go around the block or to a street wide and clear enough for
the purpose.
(Prior Code, § 521.02) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917; Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.17 EMERGING FROM ALLEYS, BUILDINGS.
   The operator of a vehicle emerging from an alley, driveway, or building
shall stop such vehicle prior to driving onto a sidewalk or onto the sidewalk
area extending across an alleyway.
(Prior Code, § 521.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.18 DRIVING ON SIDEWALK AREAS.
   No operator of a vehicle shall drive within any sidewalk area except where
the same is used as a permanent or temporary driveway.
(Prior Code, § 521.04) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.19 DRIVING THROUGH PROCESSIONS.
   No operator of any vehicle shall drive between the vehicles comprising a
funeral or any authorized procession while such procession is conspicuously so
designated. This provision shall not apply at intersections where traffic is
controlled by traffic control signals or police officers.   
(Prior Code, § 521.05) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.20 DISPLAYING ADVERTISEMENTS.
   No person shall operate any vehicle on any street or highway for the primary
purpose of displaying advertisements unless a special permit is granted
therefor by the Borough Manager and/or his or her deisgnee.
(Prior Code, § 521.06) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
**** SAFETY AND EQUIPMENT ****
§ 72.35 DRIVING UNSAFE VEHICLES.
   No person shall drive a vehicle which is in such condition or so constructed
or loaded as to be likely to cause delay in traffic or accident or injury to
human, beast, or property.
(Prior Code, § 525.01) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.36 NOISY LOADS.
   No person shall load a vehicle with iron or other material that may strike
together, or drive a vehicle so loaded, without such material being properly
“deafened” so as to prevent unnecessary noise.
(Prior Code, § 525.02) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.37 LOOSE, SHIFTING LOADS.
   No person shall load a vehicle with sand, gravel, lime, manure, dirt,
shavings, sawdust, or other loose, sifting materials, or drive a vehicle so
loaded unless such vehicle is equipped with an absolutely tight box or other
receptacle into which such material is loaded, and no part of any such load
shall be higher than the sides and ends of the box or receptacle into which
such material is loaded.
(Prior Code, § 525.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.38 UNATTENDED MOTOR VEHICLES.
   A motor vehicle left standing without its operator in charge thereof shall
be efficiently locked, its emergency brake set, and its engine shut off.
(Prior Code, § 525.04) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.39 UNAUTHORIZED RIDERS.
   No person shall ride upon any vehicle without the driver’s consent nor with
any part of his or her body protruding therefrom.
(Prior Code, § 525.05) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.40 DENSE EXHAUST FUMES.
   No person shall operate any vehicle which, by its operation, emits dense
smoke from its exhaust pipe or from any other part of such vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 525.06) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
70.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Whoever violates §
72.15 shall be fined not more than $1,000.
(Prior Code, § 521.99) (Ord. 477, passed 5-1-1972; Ord. 592, passed 7-18-1989)
