**** PARKING GENERALLY ****
§ 73.01 PROHIBITED STANDING OR PARKING PLACES.
   No person shall stop, stand, or park any vehicle in any of the following
places, except when necessary to avoid a conflict with other traffic or in
compliance with the direction of a police officer:
   (A)   Within an intersection;
   (B)   On a crosswalk;
   (C)   On a sidewalk;
   (D)   Alongside another vehicle, or “double-parked;”
   (E)   With the left side to the curb;
   (F)   Alongside or opposite any street excavation or obstruction when such
stopping, standing, or parking would obstruct traffic;
   (G)   In front of any entrance to any public hall, theater, or church, or
any place of like nature, while the same is being occupied, except to take on
or discharge passengers;
   (H)   At any place where no-parking signs appear;
   (I)   Any other place where parking is prohibited by the Vehicle Code of the
state, being 75 Pa.C.S. §§ 101 et seq.; or
   (J)   Within ten feet of a fire hydrant.
(Prior Code, § 551.01) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917; Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.02 BUS STOPS AND TAXICAB STANDS.
   (A)   Council may, by resolution, establish bus stops and taxicab stands on
such public streets and highways, in such places and in such numbers as it
determines to be of the greatest convenience to the public, and every bus stop
and taxicab stand shall be designated by appropriate signs.
   (B)   No person shall stand or park any vehicle other than a bus in any
officially designated bus stop, and no person shall stand or park any vehicle
other than a taxicab in any officially designated taxicab stand, except that
the operator of any passenger vehicle may temporarily stop in such stop or
stand for the purpose of and while actually engaged in the loading or unloading
of passengers.
   (C)   No operator of any bus or taxicab shall stand or park such bus or
taxicab upon any street or highway in any business district at any place other
than at a bus stop or taxicab stand, respectively, except that this provision
shall not prevent the operator of any such vehicle from temporarily stopping
such vehicle in accordance with other parking regulations at any place for the
purpose of and while actually engaged in loading or unloading passengers.
(Prior Code, § 551.02) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.03 PARKING WITHOUT LICENSE PLATES.
   No person shall park on any street or highway any vehicle which does not
have displayed thereon a proper license plate or plates.
(Prior Code, § 551.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.04 VEHICLES EXCEEDING 23 FEET.
   No person shall park any vehicle exceeding 23 feet in length within the
Business District or the area bounded by Third Avenue and Fifth Avenue, and by
Eighth Street and Tenth Street, inclusive. However, this provision shall not
prevent the operator of any such vehicle from stopping to actually load or
unload or from temporarily waiting to be serviced at a service station.
(Prior Code, § 551.04) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.05 FIRE ZONES.
   During a fire of a building or any alarm of fire, the particular section or
block of the street or streets in which the same is situated shall be a fire
zone, and no motor vehicle shall be operated or parked in such zones during the
progress of the fire or emergency.
(Prior Code, § 551.05) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.06 PARALLEL PARKING REQUIRED; EXCEPTIONS.
   Except when necessary in obedience to traffic regulations or traffic signs
or signals, no operator of any vehicle shall park, stand, or stop such vehicle
in a street or highway other than parallel with the edge of the street or
highway headed in the direction of traffic, and with the curb side wheels of
the vehicle on the street or highway and within six inches of the edge of the
street or highway, except as provided in the following:
   (A)   Upon those streets or highways which have been marked or assigned for
angle parking, vehicles shall be parked at an angle to the curb indicated by
such marks or signs;
   (B)   Vehicles may stand backed up to the curb while actually loading or
unloading, and in such case no part of such vehicles shall extend towards the
center of the street more than 14 feet from the curb line. However, the
operator of such vehicle shall first obtain permission from the Code
Enforcement Officer or the Chief of Police; and/or
   (C)   (1)   Whenever it appears necessary to the owner or operator of a
motor or other vehicle to protect life or property, he or she may park such
vehicle by driving over the curb on the right-hand side of any street or
highway.
      (2)   However, he or she shall be liable for any property damage caused
by his or her parking over the curb.
(Prior Code, § 551.06) (Ord. 303, passed 1-18-1954) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.07 ANGLE PARKING.
   (A)   Council shall determine upon what streets or highways angle parking
shall be permitted and shall cause the same to be marked for that purpose.
   (B)   Except where angle parking is allowed, no person shall park or stand
any vehicle at any angle backed up or head on to a curb except while actually
loading or unloading, and then only as provided in §
73.06(B).
(Prior Code, § 551.07) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.08 DISPLAYING VEHICLE FOR SALE OR RENT.
   No person shall park any vehicle upon a street or highway for the purpose of
advertising its sale or rental.   
(Prior Code, § 551.08) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.09 OBSTRUCTION OF CROSSWALKS.
   No person shall stop, stand, or park any vehicle in any street or highway so
as to obstruct a crossing or crosswalk, except to allow another vehicle or
pedestrian to cross its path.   
(Prior Code, § 551.09) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.10 OBSTRUCTION OF TRAFFIC.
   No person shall occupy any street or highway with any vehicle so as to
interfere with or interrupt the passage of other vehicles thereon.
(Prior Code, § 551.10) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.11 PRIORITY OF VEHICLES PICKING UP OR DISCHARGING PASSENGERS.
   No operator of any vehicle which is parked in front of an entrance to a
building shall fail to promptly give way to a vehicle arriving to pick up or
discharge passengers.
(Prior Code, § 551.11) (Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917) Penalty, see §
70.99
**** PARKING METERS ****
§ 73.25 METER ZONES AND FEES.
   (A)   Council, by resolution, shall establish and designate portions of the
public streets and highways of the borough as parking meter zones.
   (B)   All parking meters within the borough will be effective and require
payment of fees from Monday through Friday between the hours of 9:00 a.m. and
5:00 p.m.
(Prior Code, § 553.01) (Ord. 408, passed 3-16-1962; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987;
Ord. 645, passed 9-9-2002)
§ 73.26 INSTALLATION AND PERFORMANCE OF METERS.
   (A)   On streets and highways designated as parking meter zones, the Public
Works Director, Borough Manager and/or their designee or other person
designated by Council shall cause parking spaces to be marked off, on the curb
or pavement, or by other appropriate means, and in each space so marked shall
erect or cause to be erected a parking meter, which upon the deposit of a coin
or combination of coins of the United States in an amount indicated on the
meter, will entitle the user to park his or her car for a limited time as
indicated on the meter.
   (B)   The meter shall also indicate when the authorized parking time has
expired.
(Prior Code, § 553.02) (Ord. 238, passed 6-2-1947; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
§ 73.27 USE OF METERS.
   (A)   (1)   In order that authorized persons may properly compute the time
during which a vehicle is parked, the owner or operator of such vehicle shall,
upon entering such parking space, during the time of limited parking,
immediately deposit a coin or combination of coins of the United States in the
parking meter situated at the side of such parking space, and operate same
according to instructions thereon.
      (2)   Failure to do so shall constitute a violation of this subchapter.
      (3)   Upon the deposit of such coin and placing the meter in operation,
the parking space may be lawfully occupied by such vehicle during the period of
parking time which has been prescribed for the particular amount deposited.
   (B)   (1)   When a vehicle shall be unlawfully parked as provided in this
section, any person authorized to do so by the borough shall issue a ticket or
citation containing a notice that such vehicle was parked in violation of this
subchapter, and designating the license number of the vehicle involved in such
violation, the time when such vehicle was parked in violation of this
subchapter, and the amount of the penalty of said violation.
      (2)   The penalty for violating this section shall be determined by
Borough Council by resolution from time to time.
   (C)   If a vehicle remains parked in any such parking space for such length
of time as the meter indicates by a proper signal that the lawful parking
period has expired, such vehicle shall be considered as parking overtime, and
the parking of a vehicle overtime shall be a violation of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 553.03) (Ord. 238, passed 6-2-1947; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.28 PARKING WITHIN LINES OR MARKINGS.
   No person shall park any vehicle across any line or marking designating a
parking space, or park any vehicle in any way that the same shall not be wholly
within a parking space as designated by such lines or markings.
(Prior Code, § 553.04) (Ord. 238, passed 6-2-1947; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.29 USE OF SLUGS AND THE LIKE.
   No person shall deposit or cause to be deposited in any parking meter a
slug, device, or metallic substitute for a coin of the United States.
(Prior Code, § 553.05) (Ord. 238, passed 6-2-1947; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.30 TAMPERING WITH METERS.
   No person shall deface, tamper with, damage, open, or willfully break,
destroy, or impair the usefulness of any parking meter installed under the
terms of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 553.06) (Ord. 238, passed 6-2-1947; Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 73.31 FURTHER VIOLATION.
   In the event that a violation has not been corrected within one hour after
the issuance of a ticket or citation, a further ticket or citation may be
issued for a violation of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 553.07) (Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
§ 73.32 RATES.
   Council shall, by resolution, determine the amount of time a vehicle may be
parked upon deposit into a parking meter of a particular coin.
(Prior Code, § 553.08) (Ord. 568, passed 6-16-1987)
CHAPTER 74: PARKING AND TRAFFIC SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   School zones
      
II.   Commercial and heavy vehicles
      
III.   One-way streets
      
IV.   Stop intersections
      
V.   Authority of Council; signs; limited and prohibited parking places
      
VI.   Permit parking
SCHEDULE I. SCHOOL ZONES.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this schedule, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      BOROUGH. The Borough of Ford City, Armstrong County, Pennsylvania.
      MOTOR VEHICLE. Any self-propelled vehicle, including any device in upon
or by which any person or property is or may be transported upon a highway,
including, but not limited to, a car, van, truck, bus, or motorcycle.
      SCHOOL DAY. A day when the school is open for the attendance of the
children.
   (B)   School zones. The remaining school zone is for the Divine Redeemer
School. Any other school zone will be as set by Council moving forward.
   (C)   Penalty.
      (1)   Any person who shall violate the provisions of this schedule shall
be cited with a violation of 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 3365(b) and 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 3362 of
the State Motor Vehicle Code, which now reads as follows: three points and a
fine in an amount not to exceed $1,000.
      (2)   Any person exceeding the maximum speed amount by more than five mph
shall pay an additional fine of $2 per mile for each mile in excess of five mph
over the maximum speed limit.
(Prior Code, § 523.99)
(Ord. 569, passed 11-17-1987; Ord. 592, passed 7-18-1989)
SCHEDULE II. COMMERCIAL AND HEAVY VEHICLES.
   (A)   Truck route established. There is hereby established in the borough a
truck route which all trucks and motor vehicles, whose capacity exceeds one and
one-half tons, shall use in traversing the public ways of the borough, except
as hereinafter provided.
(Prior Code, § 527.01)
   (B)   Description of route. The truck route shall commence at the northern
end of Fifth Avenue at the intersection of Seventeenth Street, and shall extend
south along Fifth Avenue to the southern boundary of the borough. However,
trucks may enter the Borough from its eastern limits on Hill Road, until the
point where Hill Road joins with Fifth Avenue.
(Prior Code, § 527.02)
   (C)   Use of route; exceptions. All trucks or motor vehicles whose capacity
exceeds one and one-half tons shall hereafter be required to use the truck
route for passing through the borough or for the hauling of any substances,
whether merchandise or material, within the borough, or from any point within
the borough to any point without the borough, or vice-versa, except that when
any such truck or motor vehicle contains either merchandise or material which
is to be delivered to or hauled from any point within the borough not located
on the truck route, such truck or motor vehicle shall be permitted to leave the
truck route on the street nearest its destination and proceed by the most
direct route to such destination using the streets or avenues of the borough.
Immediately after being loaded or unloaded, such truck or motor vehicle shall
be returned to the truck route by the most direct route using the streets or
avenues of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 527.03)
   (D)   Signs. The Public Works Director, Borough Manager and/or their
designee shall cause neat, legible signs to be erected along the truck route
designating it as such and warning all truck drivers that violations of the
provisions of this schedule will result in a fine or imprisonment, as provided
in §
70.99.
(Prior Code, § 527.04)
(Ord. 356, passed 8-10-1956) Penalty, see §
70.99
SCHEDULE III. ONE-WAY STREETS.
   Council may, by resolution from time to time, designate the streets or
highways upon which vehicular traffic shall move or turn only in one direction.
A sign indicating the direction of traffic or turn shall be erected and
maintained at every intersection where movement of such traffic is so
regulated.
(Prior Code, § 511.04) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945; Res. 319, passed 9-13-1954;
Ord. 630-A, passed 6-3-1996; Ord. 673, passed 10-1-2007) Penalty, see §
70.99
SCHEDULE IV. STOP INTERSECTIONS.
   (A)   Intersections.
      (1)   Fourth Avenue.
         (a)   Pursuant to authority of 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 6109(a)(6), as amended,
the driver of a vehicle shall be required to stop at the following locations
before proceeding:
            1.   The driver of a vehicle traveling north on Fourth Avenue shall
stop at the southerly side of the Fourth Avenue-Twelfth Street intersection
before entering the intersection; and
            2.   The driver of a vehicle traveling south on Fourth Avenue shall
stop at the northerly side of the Fourth Avenue-Twelfth Street intersection
before entering the intersection.
         (b)   Pursuant to authority of 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 6124, Erection of
Traffic Control Devices at Intersections Act No. 81, § 1, of June 17, 1976,
P.L. No. 162 effective July 1, 1977, as amended, it is hereby directed that
three stop signs be erected at Hill Street and Short Street of the borough, to
direct that all vehicular traffic stop at this intersection before proceeding.
      (2)   Sixth Avenue. Pursuant to authority 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 6109(a)(6), as
amended, the driver of a vehicle shall be required to stop at the following
locations before proceeding:
         (a)   The driver of a vehicle traveling north on Sixth Avenue shall
stop at the southerly side of the Sixth Avenue–Ninth Street intersection before
entering the intersection; and
         (b)   The driver of a vehicle traveling south on Sixth Avenue shall
stop at the northerly side of the Sixth Avenue–Ninth Street intersection before
entering the intersection.
      (3)   Eighth Street. Pursuant to authority 75 Pa.C.S.A. § 6109(a)(6), as
amended, the driver of a vehicle shall be required to stop at the following
locations before proceeding:
         (a)   The driver of a vehicle traveling north on Third Avenue shall
stop at the southerly side of the Third Avenue–Eighth Street intersection
before entering the intersection; and
         (b)   The driver of a vehicle traveling south on Third Avenue shall
stop at the northerly side of the Third Avenue–Eighth Street intersection
before entering the intersection.
   (B)   Penalty. The penalties for violating the provisions of this section
shall be specified by the State Vehicle Code.
   (C)   Signs. Proper signs which conform to the requirements of the State
Department of Transportation shall be erected at the locations specified
herein.
(Prior Code, § 511.08) (Ord. 622, passed 7-1-1995; Ord. 653, passed 5-5-2003;
Ord. 667, passed 11-6-2006; Ord. 683, passed 7-6-2009) Penalty, see §
70.99
SCHEDULE V. AUTHORITY OF COUNCIL; SIGNS; LIMITED AND PROHIBITED PARKING PLACES.
   (A)   Council may, by resolution from time to time, designate the streets or
highways, or any part or side thereof, upon which parking shall be prohibited
or limited as to time.
   (B)   No person shall stop, stand, or park any vehicle within any portion of
a street or highway for a longer period of time than that designated by signs
erected upon such street or highway by the borough.
   (C)   With the exception of pick-up trucks, United States military vehicles,
or vehicles parked in permitted areas, no person shall park a truck, truck
tractor, truck camper, trailer, camper, any motor vehicle designed for and used
to provide a mobile swelling, sleeping, office or commercial space, or any
boat, watercraft, and trailers designed to transport same, upon any street
located within the borough.
(Prior Code, § 551.12)
(Ord. 74, passed 6-5-1917; Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945; Res. 296, passed 7-6-
1953; Res. 304, passed 1-18-1954; Res. 315, passed 6-7-1954; Res. 334, passed
7-5-1955; Res. 336, passed 8-1-1955; Res. 337, passed 9-12-1955; Res. 343,
passed 12-5-1955; Res. 346, passed 3-5-1956; Ord. 618, passed 4-19-1994; Ord.
623-B, passed 9-19-1995; Ord. 639, passed 1-8-2001; Ord. 644, passed 4-1-2002;
Ord. 652, passed 5-5-2003; Ord. 659-2, passed 4-4-2005; Ord. 670, passed 6-18-
2007) Penalty, see §
70.99
SCHEDULE VI. PERMIT PARKING.
   (A)   Permit parking only zones. A permit parking zone is hereby established
for both the west and east sides of Third Avenue starting at Joe Cippel Way and
ending at Eleventh Street, Monday through Friday, between the hours 8:00 a.m.
and 5:00 p.m.
(Prior Code, § 555.01)
   (B)   Permit parking sub-zones. The parking zone defined in division (A)
above shall be further divided into sub-zones. The said sub-zones are
established as follows:
      (1)   Sub-zone 1. Both the west and east sides of Third Avenue from Joe
Cippel Way to Tenth Street;
      (2)   Sub-zone 2. Both the west and east sides of Third Avenue from Tenth
Street to O’Conner Street; and
      (3)   Sub-zone 3. The west side of Third Avenue from O’Conner Street to
Eleventh Street.
(Prior Code, § 555.02)
   (C)   Fees.
      (1)   Any fees will be as set from time to time by Council via
resolution.
      (2)   The fees in division (C)(1) above are initial fees and may be
changed from time to time by resolution of the Borough Council. During the days
of the week and during the times provided for in division (A) above, only
persons who have a valid parking permit for a sub-zone shall be permitted to
park a vehicle in that sub-zone, and then, only in their designated place.
(Prior Code, § 555.03)
   (D)   Signage and labels. Appropriate signage, stenciling, and vehicle
labels shall be prepared and installed, as applicable, to accomplish the
purposes of this schedule.
(Prior Code, § 555.04)
   (E)   Penalty.
      (1)   Whoever violates any provision of this schedule shall be guilty of
a summary offense and fined not more than $300 per day or violators shall pay
the costs of prosecution. In default of this payment of such fines and costs,
the violator shall be imprisoned not more than 30 days.
      (2)   Anyone who violates any provision of divisions (A) through (D)
above, except as provided above, shall be guilty of a summary offense and shall
be subject to be fined in an amount not to exceed $300 per day and costs of
prosecution. In default of the payment of such fines and costs, the violator
shall be imprisoned for not more than 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 555.99)
(Ord. 573, passed 12-15-1987; Ord. 644, passed 4-1-2002; Ord. 670, passed 6-18-
2007)

**** ANIMALS GENERALLY ****
§ 90.01 REMOVAL OF DEAD ANIMALS.
   The carcass of any animal which has died within the borough shall be removed
within 24 hours and properly disposed of by the owner of the animal if the
owner is known, or by the owner of the property on which the dead animal is
found. If the ownership is unknown and the carcass is found on a street, alley,
or public place, it shall be removed by the Borough Code Enforcement Officer in
accordance with the laws of the commonwealth.
(Prior Code, § 701.01) (Ord. 67, passed 8-4-1914) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.02 HOGS AND PENS.
   No hog pens shall be constructed or maintained within the borough, and no
hogs shall be kept within the borough.
(Prior Code, § 701.02) (Ord. 67, passed 8-4-1914; Ord. 248, passed 9-14-1948)
Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.03 ANIMALS UPON STREETS.
   No animal shall be left unattended on any street or highway unless securely
fastened, and no person shall stop or stand any animal on any street for a
longer period than one hour.
(Prior Code, § 701.03) (Ord. 232, passed 8-21-1945) Penalty, see §
90.99
**** DOGS ****
§ 90.15 COMPLIANCE, LICENSING REQUIRED.
   All persons, owning, keeping, or having any dog in the borough shall comply
with all state laws providing for the licensing and care of such dog and will
control or contain such dog so that the dog will not run at large beyond the
private property of the owner or keeper of the dog.
(Prior Code, § 707.01) (Ord. 476, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.16 CONTROL OF ANIMAL IN PUBLIC PLACE.
   No person shall permit any dog owned or controlled by him or her to excrete
or deposit any bodily waste or filth in or upon the avenues, streets, alleys,
or ways, parks, or public playgrounds or upon land of other persons.
(Prior Code, § 707.02) (Ord. 476, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.17 RESTRICTED IN BUSINESS DISTRICT.
   All dogs shall be restricted from the streets or sidewalks of the Business
District of the borough, bounded by Third Avenue on the west, O’Connor Street
on the north, Fifth Avenue on the east, and Eighth Street on the south.
(Prior Code, § 707.03) (Ord. 476, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.18 LEASH REQUIRED.
   No person shall walk a dog in public without a leash.
(Prior Code, § 707.04) (Ord. 476, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
90.99
**** CHICKENS, SWINE AND OTHER FARM ANIMALS ****
§ 90.30 AUTHORITY.
   This subchapter is adopted pursuant to the borough’s authority under 8
Pa.C.S. § 1202 to regulate animals, abate nuisances, protect public health and
preserve the residential character of the borough. This subchapter applies
borough-wide regardless of zoning district.
§ 90.31 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter is to prevent nuisances, protect public
health and safety, reduce odor and vermin issues, avoid property damage and
maintain safe and sanitary conditions throughout the borough.
§ 90.32 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning. All definitions shall
be interpreted broadly and inclusively to ensure full regulatory coverage.
   ANIMAL. Any live creature, whether domesticated, companion, livestock,
avian, reptile or amphibian, excluding humans.
   CHICKEN. A domesticated fowl (Gallus gallus domesticus) of any age,
including hens and pullets. This term excludes roosters.
   COOP. A fully enclosed, predator-resistant structure designed for housing
hens, with a roof, floor, solid walls or secure wire walls and protected
openings for ventilation.
   FARM ANIMAL. Any animal ordinarily associated with agricultural or livestock
operations, including, but not limited to: chickens, ducks, geese, turkeys,
gamebirds, pigeons, peafowl, goats, sheep, cattle, horses, donkeys, mules,
llamas, alpacas, bees, rabbits or similar species, whether kept for personal
use, hobby use or commercial use, including miniature, dwarf or specialty
breeds.
   HEN. A female chicken of any age.
   HOG or SWINE. Any member of the species Sus scrofa domesticus, including
potbellied pigs, miniature pigs, “micro-pigs,” or any breed or variety marketed
as a companion or household pet. Size, age, or marketing description does not
exempt a hog or swine from this definition.
   OWNER or KEEPER. Any individual, household or entity that owns, harbors,
keeps, maintains, controls or permits an animal on their property.
   PROPERTY. Any parcel of land, lot or structure within the borough.
   ROOSTER. A male chicken of any age, including cockerels.
   RUN. An outdoor enclosure fenced and secured on all sides, including the top
if necessary, that prevents hens from escaping and prevents access by
predators.
§ 90.33 PROHIBITED ANIMALS.
   (A)   Farm animals. All farm animals are prohibited within the borough,
except hens kept in compliance with §
90.34. This includes all livestock, poultry, waterfowl, small gamebirds and
similar species.
   (B)   Roosters. Roosters are prohibited borough-wide without exception.
§ 90.34 PERMITTED HENS.
   Hens may be kept only under all of the following conditions:
   (A)   Maximum number. No more than six hens may be kept on a property.
   (B)   Eligible properties. Hens may only be kept on lots improved with a
single-family detached dwelling. Hens are prohibited on:
      (1)   Duplexes;
      (2)   Townhouses;
      (3)   Multi-family dwellings;
      (4)   Apartments;
      (5)   Mixed-use buildings;
      (6)   Vacant lots; and
      (7)   Commercial or industrial properties.
   (C)   Location requirements. All hens, coops and runs must be located:
      (1)   Entirely within the rear yard;
      (2)   At least ten feet from all property lines;
      (3)   At least 25 feet from any dwelling on an adjacent lot; and
      (4)   Outside easements, stormwater facilities and drainage areas.
   (D)   Coop requirements.
      (1)   Coops must be fully enclosed, predator-proof, structurally sound
and weather-resistant.
      (2)   Coops shall not exceed 100 square feet unless otherwise approved
under Borough Code.
      (3)   Coops must be kept clean, sanitary and free from accumulated waste,
standing water or odors.
   (E)   Run requirements.
      (1)   Runs must be securely fenced to prevent escape or predator entry.
      (2)   Hens must remain within the coop or run at all times. Free-range
activity is prohibited.
   (F)   Food and waste management.
      (1)   Feed must be stored in sealed, rodent-proof containers.
      (2)   Waste must be removed regularly and disposed of in a sanitary
manner.
      (3)   Waste shall not be allowed to accumulate, generate odors, attract
vermin or create runoff.
   (G)   Slaughtering. Outdoor slaughtering is prohibited.
§ 90.35 NUISANCES.
   The keeping of hens shall not create odors, noise, waste accumulation,
insects, vermin, runoff or unsanitary conditions. Any such condition is a
violation of this subchapter regardless of compliance with §
90.34.
§ 90.36 INSPECTIONS.
   The borough may conduct inspections, with reasonable notice, to ensure
compliance with this subchapter.
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.
CHAPTER 91: HEALTH, SAFETY, AND SANITATION

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 91.01 ABANDONED VEHICLES.
   (A)   Parking or repairing on streets. No person shall abandon any motor
vehicle on the public avenues, streets, or alleys, or park or permit to be
parked any disabled motor vehicle or vehicle which does not bear current
inspection and registration plates, upon the avenues, streets, or alleys of the
borough, or repair or permit to be repaired a motor vehicle while parked on the
avenues, streets, or alleys of the borough, except for such emergency service
as is necessary to move the vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 703.01)
   (B)   Gasoline, grease, oil, and the like on streets. No person shall
deposit or permit to be deposited any gasoline, grease, oil, hydraulic fluid,
or radiator coolant upon the surface of the avenues, streets, or alleys of the
borough, either directly or from a motor vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 703.02)
(Ord. 481, passed 5-1-1972) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.02 SLAUGHTERING ANIMALS.
   (A)   Slaughter, gutting, rendering, and storage prohibited. No person shall
willfully kill or slaughter, within the borough, any animal, the meat of which
is intended or is offered for sale for human consumption, either within or
without the borough. No person shall remove the intestines or hides of any such
slain animal, render lard or tallow from the carcass of such slain animal, or
store or keep in storage in any place within the borough the hides of any such
slain animal.
(Prior Code, § 1109.01)
   (B)   Abatement. Any violation of the provisions of this section shall be
deemed and is hereby declared to be a public nuisance, and the proper officers
or agents of the borough are hereby authorized and empowered to enter and
prosecute such actions, either at law or in equity, as may be necessary to
suppress or abate such nuisance. Such remedy shall be in addition to any
penalty provided in §
91.99(C).
(Prior Code, § 1109.02)
(Ord. 248, passed 9-14-1948) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.03 OPEN BURNING OF NATURAL MATERIALS.
   (A)   No person shall burn any material in the open within the borough
except natural, non-noxious materials consisting only of leaves, paper,
cardboard, and untreated, unpainted, and uncoated wood. The burning of trash,
refuse, household garbage, construction debris, plastics, chemicals, or any
noxious or synthetic material is strictly prohibited. Open burning of permitted
natural materials may occur only between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 a.m.
and between the hours of 4:00 p.m. and sunset on Tuesdays, Wednesdays,
Thursdays, Fridays, and Saturdays of each week. Even during such hours,
permitted materials shall be burned on private premises only and shall be
placed in a suitable container so as to prevent sparks, burning embers, ashes,
or other charred materials from being blown or scattered.
   (B)   Within a reasonable time following the permissible burning periods,
all smoldering materials emitting smoke or other offensive odors shall be
smothered or quenched with water to eliminate such smoke or odors.
   (C)   At no time shall any material be set afire or burned on any of the
avenues, streets, alleys, thoroughfares, parks, or playgrounds of the borough.
§ 91.04 LITTERING.
   No person shall throw or cause to be thrown on any streets, alleys, or other
public places of the borough any paper, tin cans, broken glass, stones, nails,
or other rubbish.
(Prior Code, § 721.02) (Ord. 24, passed 4-22-1903; Ord. 67, passed 1-4-1914;
Ord. 227, passed 12-4-1944) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.05 SNOW REMOVAL; RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNERS AND OCCUPANTS.
   No owner or occupant of any lot or part of a lot within the borough shall
fail, within six hours after any fall or deposit of snow and sleet, to remove
the same from the pavements and sidewalks fronting the premises respectively
owned or occupied by him or her.
(Prior Code, § 721.03) (Ord. 63, passed 2-2-1914) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.06 ABANDONED REFRIGERATORS.
   No person shall leave or permit to remain outside of any dwelling, building,
or other structure or within any unoccupied or abandoned dwelling or building
under his or her control, in a place accessible to children, any abandoned or
unattended or discarded icebox, refrigerator, or other container which has an
airtight or discarded snap-lock, or other locking device which may not be
released from the inside of any such container, unless he or she has first
removed, destroyed, or broken the door or lid, snap-lock, or other locking
device from such icebox, refrigerator, or other container.   
(Prior Code, § 721.06) (Ord. 317, passed 7-15-1954) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.07 WASHING AUTOMOBILES.
   No person shall wash or cause to be washed upon any street, avenue, or
public thoroughfare of the borough, either gratuitously or for hire, any motor
vehicle. However, this section shall not extend to the washing of any motor
vehicle by the owner thereof in front of his or her own premises or property,
whether the same be owned in fee or leased.
(Prior Code, § 721.07) (Ord. 160, passed 4-5-1926) Penalty, see §
91.99
**** NUISANCES GENERALLY ****
§ 91.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   NUISANCE. Shall include, but not be limited to, accumulations of garbage and
rubbish, storage of abandoned or junked motor vehicles, dilapidated or unsturdy
structures which pose a danger to public health or safety, on public or private
grounds, and the carrying on of an offensive manufacture or business. Also
includes that as set by Borough Council.
   PERSON. Includes all natural persons, associations, firms, partnerships, and
corporations.
(Prior Code, § 731.01) (Ord. 521, passed 11-13-1978)
§ 91.16 MADE UNLAWFUL.
   After the effective date of this subchapter, it shall be unlawful for any
person to maintain or permit to be maintained any nuisance within the limits of
the borough.
(Prior Code, § 731.02) (Ord. 521, passed 11-13-1978) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.17 NOTICE TO REMOVE OR REMEDY.
   (A)   Notwithstanding §
91.99(E), the Council of the borough may give notice to the owner of the public
or private grounds of which a nuisance is maintained or allowed to be
maintained to remove or remedy the nuisance within 30 days of receipt of such
notice. Notice shall be sent to the owner at his or her residence by certified
mail.
   (B)   In the event the owner fails to remove or remedy the nuisance within
30 days of receipt of notice to do so, the Council of the borough may remove or
remedy the nuisance or may institute proceedings in a Court of Equity to secure
the relief which the Court may deem just and proper.
(Prior Code, § 731.03) (Ord. 521, passed 11-13-1978)
§ 91.18 REMOVAL OR REMEDY BY COUNCIL.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.
**** PUBLIC WASTE ****
§ 91.30 NUISANCE CONDITIONS UPON PRIVATE PROPERTY; ABATEMENT.
   No person shall maintain or permit to be maintained on any property owned or
occupied by him or her within the borough any condition productive of a
nuisance, detrimental to the public health or which may be so declared by the
Borough Code Enforcement Officer. Upon receipt of a notice from the Borough
Code Enforcement Officer that such a condition exists on the property owned or
occupied by such person, it shall be his or her duty to abate the nuisance
created thereby to the satisfaction of the Borough Code Enforcement Officer
within the time specified in the notice.
(Prior Code, § 1103.02) (Ord. 67, passed 8-4-1914) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.31 DEPOSIT OF WASTE UPON PUBLIC WAYS.
   No person shall place upon any public street or alley any organic matter
subject to decomposition, either vegetable or animal, or allow kitchen waste
from any property owned or occupied by him or her to run into any alley,
street, or gutter along any street or alley, or to accumulate upon the surface
of the ground in any place in such manner as to be offensive or to constitute a
menace to health.
(Prior Code, § 1103.03) (Ord. 67, passed 8-4-1914) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.32 SPITTING IN OR UPON PUBLIC PLACES.
   No person shall spit on any sidewalk, in any public conveyance, or on the
floor or steps of any public hall or building.   
(Prior Code, § 1103.04) (Ord. 67, passed 8-4-1914) Penalty, see §
91.99
**** WEEDS ****
§ 91.45 VEGETATION NUISANCE UNDER CERTAIN CONDITIONS.
   No person, firm, or corporation owning or occupying any property in the
borough shall permit:
   (A)   Any grass or weeds or any vegetation whatsoever, not edible or planted
for some useful or ornamental purpose, to grow or remain upon such premises so
as to exceed the height of six inches or to throw off any unpleasant or noxious
odor or to conceal any filthy deposit or to create or produce pollen. Any
grass, weeds, or other vegetation growing upon any premises in the borough in
violation of any provision of this section is hereby declared to be a nuisance
and detrimental to the health, safety, cleanliness, and comfort of the
inhabitants of the borough; or
   (B)   Any dead trees or any dead portion of a tree to remain upon such
premises. Any such dead tree or any dead portion thereof upon any premises in
the borough in violation of any provision of this section is hereby declared to
be a nuisance and detrimental to the health, safety, cleanliness, and comfort
of the inhabitants of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 1111.01) (Ord. 624, passed 7-1-1995) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.46 RESPONSIBILITY FOR REMOVAL.
   The owner of any premises, as to a vacant premises or premises occupied by
the owner and in case of multiple-unit property having more than one rental
unit, and the occupant in case of single-unit premises occupied or rented by
other than the owner thereof, shall remove, trim, or cut all grass, weeds, or
other vegetation and dead trees or portions thereof growing or remaining upon
such premises in violation of the provisions of §
91.45.
(Prior Code, § 1111.02) (Ord. 624, passed 7-1-1995) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.47 NOTICE TO REMOVE, TRIM, OR CUT; COST OF ABATEMENT BY BOROUGH.
   The Borough Council, or any officer or employee of the borough designated
thereby for the purpose, is hereby authorized to give notice, by personal
service or by United States mail, to the owner or occupant, as the case may be,
of any premises whereon grass, weeds, or other vegetation and dead trees or
portions thereof is growing or remaining in violation of the provisions of §
91.45, directing and requiring such owner or occupant to remove, trim, or cut
such grass, weeds, or other vegetation and dead trees or portions thereof, so
as to conform to the requirements of this subchapter, within five days after
issuance of such notice. In case any person, firm, or corporation shall
neglect, fail, or refuse to comply with such notice, within the period of time
stated therein, the borough authorities may remove, trim, or cut such grass,
weeds, or other vegetation and dead trees or portions thereof, and the cost
thereof, with an additional amount of 10%, may be collected by the borough from
such person, firm, or corporation in the manner provided by law.
(Prior Code, § 1111.03) (Ord. 624, passed 7-1-1995)
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate any provision of this code
of ordinances or fails to comply therewith or with any of the requirements
thereof, upon conviction thereof in an action brought before a Magisterial
District Judge in the manner provided for the enforcement of summary offenses
under the Pennsylvania Rules of Criminal Procedure, shall be sentenced to pay a
fine of not less than $100, nor more than $1,000, plus costs, including
reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred by the borough and, in default of payment
of said fines and costs, to a term of imprisonment to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses. A separate offense shall arise for
each day or portion thereof in which a violation of this code of ordinances is
found to exist or for each section of this code of ordinances found to have
been violated. The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity to
prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin or abate violations of this code of
ordinances. All fines and penalties collected for violation of this code of
ordinances shall be paid to the borough.
CHAPTER 92: PARKS AND PLAYGROUNDS
Section
   
92.01   Statement of policy
   
92.02   Presently existing parks
   
92.03   Certain acts prohibited
   
92.04   Hours of operation
   
92.05   Rules and regulations authorized
   
92.06   Fees for use and rental of borough park and recreation facilities
 
   
92.99   Penalty
§ 92.01 STATEMENT OF POLICY.
   It is the policy of the borough that the borough-owned parks, playground,
playfields, and recreational areas (collectively the “parks”) shall be kept for
the healthful and varied amusement and recreation of the public on a year-round
basis, as a means of promoting the general health and welfare. Use to the
maximum intensity is to be encouraged, subject to requirements of health,
safety, and security of the parks. This chapter shall apply to all parks now
owned by the borough, as well as those hereafter acquired; provided that
nothing set forth herein is intended to be in conflict with or inconsistent
with the requirement of any federal or state regulation appurtenant to use of
any facility acquired or developed with federal or state funds as relates to
the use of such facility.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 92.02 PRESENTLY EXISTING PARKS.
   (A)    The parks owned by the borough are:
      (1)   Ford City Memorial Park: Fourth Avenue at its intersection with
Eighth and Ninth Street;
      (2)   Boulder Park: Sixth Avenue;
      (3)   Malec Park: Seventh Avenue;
      (4)   Ford View Park: 330 Ferndale Street; and
      (5)   Softball fields: 1700 Ford City Road.
   (B)   The PARKS, as the term is defined herein, shall specifically include
the parks and playfields referenced in division (A) above, and any additional
parks or recreational areas acquired by the borough in the future.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 92.03 CERTAIN ACTS PROHIBITED.
   The following acts done within the limits of the parks are hereby declared
to be unlawful, and the Borough Police Department shall have the power to
remove any individual from the parks for violation of borough ordinances and
posted park regulations or for performing any of the acts listed here:
   (A)   To injure, deface, or destroy any posted notice, rules, or regulations
relating to the use of the parks;
   (B)   To mark, cut, injure, remove, deface, destroy, disfigure, tamper,
displace, or remove any wood, turf, grass, tree, soil, rock, sand, gravel,
shelter house, building, structure, plumbing, or electrical equipment located
in the parks;
   (C)   To make or kindle a fire in the parks;
   (D)   To indulge in riotous, boisterous, threatening, or indecent conduct,
or to use abusive, threatening, profane, or indecent language in the parks;
   (E)   To expose oneself or excrete or urinate in the parks, except in
designated restroom facilities;
   (F)   To sell, expose, or offer for sale any merchandise, article, or thing
whatsoever in the parks without first obtaining a written permit from the
borough;
   (G)   To lead, ride, let loose, or permit to run at large any animal or fowl
in the parks;
   (H)   To ride, drive, or propel any powered vehicle on a foot path, or in
any other parts of the parks, except on roadways or parking lots therein. This
prohibition does not apply to emergency vehicles or borough-owned vehicles;
   (I)   To ride or drive any powered vehicle at a speed in excess of ten mph
in the parks;
   (J)   To possess, use, carry, or discharge any fireworks or explosives in
the parks;
   (K)   To carry, transport, or consume any malt, spiritous, or vinous
beverage whatsoever within the limits of the parks, or to remain while under
the influence of the same, except during events or activities authorized by the
borough taking place in the parks;
   (L)   To sell, use, possess, or transport any illegal drugs or drug
paraphernalia in the parks;
   (M)   To deposit, drop, or cast out any trash, litter, rubbish, garbage, or
debris in the parks, except to place the same within a rubbish or trash
container provided for such purpose;
   (N)   To carry a lighted cigar, cigarette, pipe, or other lighted smoking
device in the parks, except in Ford City Memorial Park located on or about
Fourth Avenue at its intersection with Eighth and Ninth Streets; or
   (O)   To unlawfully possess, use, carry, or discharge any firearms in the
park.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.04 HOURS OF OPERATION.
   The parks shall be closed every night at dusk, prevailing time, and shall
remain closed until dawn, prevailing time, of the following day, except on
those days/nights when events or activities authorized by the borough are
taking place in the parks. During the closed period, it shall be unlawful for
any person, not having official business therein, to be within the territorial
limits of the parks. DUSK is defined as that time of day immediately following
sunset. DAWN is defined as that time of day immediately before sunrise. The
Borough Police Department shall have the power to close the parks for
violations of borough ordinances, violations of posted borough notices, rules,
and regulations, and/or for public safety concerns when circumstances, in the
discretion of the Borough Police Department, merits such adjustment.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.05 RULES AND REGULATIONS AUTHORIZED.
   Borough Council is hereby authorized to promulgate rules and regulations for
events, activities, and conduct in the parks; said rules and regulations must
be adopted by resolution of Borough Council and approved by the Borough Manager
and/or his or her designee. Any person or persons violating the rules and
regulations shall be subject to immediate dismissal from the parks. Violation
of the rules and regulations adopted and approved by Borough Council shall
constitute a violation of this chapter.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 92.06 FEES FOR USE AND RENTAL OF BOROUGH PARK AND RECREATION FACILITIES.
   Borough Council may establish, by resolution adopted from time to time, a
schedule of fees as shall be necessary for the use and/or rental of the parks.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person, firm, or corporation who violates a provision of this
chapter, or who fails to comply therewith, or with any of the requirement
thereof: shall be, upon conviction thereof, sentenced to pay a fine of not less
than $100 nor more than $1,000 for each violation, plus costs, and in default
of payment of said fine and costs, to improvement to the extent permitted by
law for the punishment of summary offenses.
   (B)   A separate offense shall arise for each day or portion thereof in
which a violation is found to exist or for each section of this chapter found
to have been violated. All fines and penalties for the violation of this
chapter shall be paid to the borough.
   (C)   The borough may also commence appropriate actions in equity or other
to prevent, restrain, correct, enjoin, or abate violations of this chapter.
(Ord. 744, passed 12-16-2019)
TITLE XI: BUSINESS REGULATIONS
   Chapter
